# Roadmap next-action round archive

**PRIOR rounds only** of `.harness/roadmap_status.md`'s `## Next action` section, most-recent-first. The CURRENT round lives ONLY in the live head — never here (R1 rule 2: a terminating refresh writes exactly one file, so this archive cannot track currency; codex round-6 resolution (b), prior-only by definition). Each entry is the round's text as it stood in the live head when superseded — its label rewritten `Current` → `Prior next action (post-#N)` at archiving, the body verbatim. Rounds archived before the U-CTX-03 truncation retain their original full-length text; rounds after it are the compact synopses the truncated head carries. Archiving happens in the NEXT content PR after a round is superseded (the superseded text is taken from git history of the live head), never in the terminating refresh commit itself — so between a terminating refresh and the next content PR, the NEWEST superseded round is not yet here; for that lag interval resolve it from the live head's own git history (`git log -p -- .harness/roadmap_status.md`), which is lossless. **Not part of any session-start, post-merge, or drift-audit read path** — resolve a specific round via targeted grep (a PR number, a `B-`/`R-` id, a round number), never by reading this file wholesale.

**Prior next action (post-#1521).** `B-235`'s operator-facing half landed at #1521: `forward_register.py --detail` now prints the canonical `close_out` from the YAML row ABOVE the prose block, separated by `PROSE_DELIMITER`, so the surface routing actually reads carries the authority instead of the hand-maintained copy alone, and `close_out` is always stated rather than rendered as silence. The row stays `registered_finding`: the detection half is deliberately not built, because the shape `B-235` originally prescribed (extract the bound letters from both bodies and compare SETS) would NOT have caught the r6 defect it was written for — at r6 the YAML asserted (c)/(d) as blocking while MENTIONING (a)/(b), so letter extraction reports agreement, and a pattern matching nothing on both sides passes VACUOUSLY. Rendering the canonical field removes the need to parse prose for the fact at all; the residual is that the two bodies can still diverge undetected, materially less dangerous now that the canonical text reaches the reader. Two things this arc produced that forward work must not lose. FIRST, `B-236` is newly registered: `leg_selfcheck`'s dirty-carrier guard snapshots `git status --porcelain` ONCE and then renders the live working tree once per row, so a concurrent write in that window is invisible — the round-11 fail-open reopened from the TIMING side. It is pre-existing on `main` (line 1122, using `_run`) and #1521 changed that one line to `_run_checked`, which is strictly safer, so it was registered rather than absorbed; its row records that the two obvious remedies do NOT hold (`git show HEAD:<path>` breaks `--uncommitted` mode, and a per-call re-check narrows without closing) and that the closure shape is DETECT-AFTER: re-run the dirty check after the row loop and fail HARD if the carriers changed. SECOND, this arc ENDED ON ITS ROUND BUDGET, NOT ON CONVERGENCE: codex r16+ and the gemini failover are both refused `GATE_REFUSED (BUDGET_EXHAUSTED)` at 15/15 because the budget is producer-agnostic, so the last three commits (the `_run_checked` fail-open fix, the `_one_line` frame-spoof fix, and the merge-gate absorption) carry NO out-of-family review and rest on the three merge-gate lenses alone — r14 and r15 each returned a real P2, one falsifying the arc's central promise and one falsifying a claim in its own PR body, so late rounds were still productive when the budget ran out; extending it is operator-gated and is the one open judgment call from this arc. **Round 15 — `B-75` CLOSED 2026-07-26 at PR #1122.** Per round 14's next-action, `advisor()` was consulted again on which of the remaining same-file candidates to build: recommended `B-75` over the still-open `B-74`/`B-77` pair — its close_out already named a known fix shape (batch-with-yield or snapshot-then-verify, no open design question), it's the P1, and `B-73` had escalated it to cross-process blast radius (candidate selection also held the cross-process flock, so a stalled scan could block other OS processes, not just threads). `gc_sweep`'s `glob()`/`stat()` candidate enumeration over `*.entry`/`.tmp-*` now runs FULLY UNLOCKED — the part whose cost scales with total store size (or, on a hanging filesystem mount, has no bound at all), and the actual hazard the row registered — producing PROVISIONAL candidates that are re-verified in a single, brief, COMBINED lock acquisition before being trusted (never one acquisition per candidate, which would have turned `_cross_process_lock`'s per-call `fcntl.flock` into N kernel round-trips). Before writing code, advisor() flagged an asymmetry to resolve first: does the fix cover `.tmp-*` too, or only `*.entry`? Worked through it by hand: a `.tmp-*` file's mtime is NOT a trustworthy "definitely done" signal unlocked (it could be mid-write) the way an entry's is (monotonic expiry + fresh uuid4 keys) — but since `tempfile.mkstemp` itself only ever runs INSIDE the lock, a temp-file candidate that survives to be re-observed once the sweep holds the lock can only be a genuine crash orphan (its creator either already finished — and already unlinked it as part of releasing the lock — or crashed while holding a lock that's now free). This closed the asymmetry: the same unlocked-scan-then-brief-relock-reverify pattern is correct and safe for BOTH file types, so the fix isn't scoped to `*.entry` only. Two new regression tests: a structural witness recording whether `_publish_lock.locked()` is true at each `glob()` call (asserts unheld, not a wall-clock check), and a stalled-enumeration concurrent-write test mirroring the existing stalled-decrypt pattern. Both mutation-probe-verified (revert the fix to the pre-fix fully-locked shape, confirm both new tests fail; restore, confirm green). Hand-traced the fix against the two most load-bearing pre-existing lock tests before writing any code, to confirm neither would regress — both passed unchanged, confirmed empirically after the fix landed. Full `harness-runtime` suite: 3087 passed, 10 skipped; ruff + pyright strict clean. `just codex-review-uncommitted`'s only findings were pre-existing untracked workspace clutter from other sessions (unrelated skill installs, a stale retired dashboard snapshot), same pattern as PR #1121 — zero findings on the actual diff. `merge-gate` 3-lens review: **all-approve at round 1** (concurrency independently re-derived both safety arguments from the actual code and confirmed them; spec-conformance independently re-ran `tools/forward_register.py --check` and the mutation probe against the real PR HEAD commit; test-witness independently RAN both new tests directly rather than reasoning-only, confirmed 0.39s combined with no timing marginality, and hand-mutation-probed both). **Next action:** 11 `registered_finding` rows remain ungrounded (`B-16`, `B-30`, `B-44`, `B-45`, `B-56`, `B-57`, `B-66`, `B-69`, `B-71`, `B-74`/`B-77` as a likely-combined pair, `B-79`/`B-80`). **Frontier:** `B-72` reproduction BUILT 2026-07-25 at PR #1107 — a real-stack test (`harness-runtime/tests/integration/test_b72_fanout_sub_agent_dispatch_hitl_gate_resume.py`) empirically confirms the fan-out HITL delivery-mechanism gap the register's round-2 spec-citation reframe predicted: a `PARALLELIZATION` branch's own `SUB_AGENT_BOUNDARY` gate correctly pauses on first dispatch, but a resolved operator answer delivered via `api.resume()` is never consumed — the gate re-fires (2nd webhook POST) instead of dispatching the child, and the run re-pauses cleanly with no state corruption but never converges. Pinned with 6 oracles including a mutation-probed direct spy on `RuntimeSubAgentDispatcher.dispatch` (0 calls before resume, 1 when the gate is bypassed — confirmed non-vacuous), not a blanket `xfail` or a weak Anthropic-client-call-count proxy (both tried and rejected across 5 codex rounds). Merge-gate 3-lens all-approve at round 1. A genuine merge conflict against the concurrently-merging B-68 PR's own terminating refresh was resolved cleanly (kept this PR's round-3 B-72 text, preserved B-68's new B-73-B-77 rows). **A separate, still-open finding**: this single-branch reproduction scopes the liveness gap but not the multi-branch addressing mechanism (the gate fires before any child run exists to key `hitl_response_for(child_run_id)` by) — needs either a multi-peer-branch reproduction or grounding on branch-own pre-dispatch identity before the fix is genuinely scoped. **Round 4 grounding (2026-07-25, doc-only, advisor()-checked twice):** re-confirmed `hitl_delivery_holder`/`hitl_delivery_cell` stay confined to `_execute_workflow_body`; `_execute_parallelization`/`_execute_orchestrator_workers` reference neither (matches round 2). The new finding: the register's own round-1 text ("dispatch entrypoint can't distinguish first-dispatch from forwarding an already-dispatched branch") and round-2 text ("a resolved answer has no delivery path") describe two DIFFERENT bug categories — dispatch-idempotency (no key needed) vs. addressing/delivery (needs a per-branch key) — and nobody had named that split. A mid-pass hypothesis floating `branch_path` as that key was `advisor()`-rejected before write-up: the single-branch repro tests zero multi-branch addressing, so "collision only hits nested grandchildren, not peers" is unverified — and it's exactly the reading the code's own keying-defect note warns against reinventing. Not a finding; flagged so it isn't independently re-derived and trusted. **Next action:** the next session (or council pass) must first resolve which bug category B-72 is — (a) addressing/delivery or (b) dispatch-idempotency — before any design or code; that answer determines whether an addressing key is needed at all. Full detail at `.harness/post-phase-8-forward-register.md` B-72 round-4 update. Treat as a fresh start, not a continuation. **Resolved this session:** `test_write_once_survives_immediate_sweep_under_slow_fsync_and_short_ttl` (B-68's own test) — the one CI failure on the post-merge run was confirmed a genuine one-off flake, not a regression: reran clean with zero code changes on the terminating-refresh commit's own CI run. `B-68` CLOSED 2026-07-25 at PR #1103 (prior frontier) — full detail at `.harness/post-phase-8-forward-register.md` B-68 through B-77.
